#=GF AU   Andreeva A

#=GF CC   are predicted to share a common fold comprising a three-bladed
#=GF CC   beta-alpha propeller. The individual blades consist of a
#=GF CC   three-stranded antiparallel beta-sheet and an alpha helix that packs

#=GF CC   coil.

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   4HB_MCP
#=GF AC   CL0457.3
#=GF DE   Aspartate chemoreceptor, signal-transduction ligand-binding
#=GF AU   Ulrich L, Coggill P
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   16306392
#=GF RT   Four-helix bundle: a ubiquitous sensory module in prokaryotic signal
#=GF RT   transduction.
#=GF RA   Ulrich LE, Zhulin IB;
#=GF RL   Bioinformatics. 2005;21:iii45-iii48.
#=GF DR   SCOP; 47170;
#=GF CC   This superfamily is characterised by a four-helix bundle structure
#=GF CC   that forms a ubiquitous sensory module in prokaryotic
#=GF CC   signal-transduction. The 4HB_MCP is always found between two
#=GF CC   predicted transmembrane helices indicating that it detects only
#=GF CC   extracellular signals. In many cases the domain is associated with a
#=GF CC   cytoplasmic HAMP domain suggesting that most proteins carrying the
#=GF CC   bundle might share the mechanism of transmembrane signalling which is
#=GF CC   well-characterised in E coli chemoreceptors.
#=GF MB   PF02203;
#=GF MB   PF05227;
#=GF MB   PF12729;
#=GF MB   PF16591;
#=GF MB   PF21689;
#=GF MB   PF27362;

#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF CC   This clan includes various families that share the BPTI-like
#=GF CC   structural fold.  The canonical member is the bovine pancreatic
#=GF CC   trypsin inhibitor (BPTI) domain, which has a characteristic fold
#=GF CC   stabilized by a conserved pattern of disulfide bonds. This fold is
#=GF CC   found in various proteins including protease inhibitors, toxins, and
#=GF CC   other proteins where a stable small domain is required. The fold is
#=GF CC   typically around 50-60 amino acids in length and is stabilized by
#=GF CC   three conserved disulfide bonds in the arrangement 1-6, 2-4, 3-5,
#=GF CC   though some members may have variations in this pattern.

#=GF ID   Bestrophin
#=GF AC   CL0897.1
#=GF DE   Bestrophin superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF CC   This clan contains families related to bestrophin proteins.
#=GF CC   Bestrophins are calcium-activated chloride channels that form
#=GF CC   pentameric assemblies in membranes. Each monomer contains multiple
#=GF CC   transmembrane helices that come together to form the ion conducting
#=GF CC   pore. The channel is gated by calcium binding to the cytoplasmic
#=GF CC   region which causes a conformational change transmitted to the pore.
#=GF CC   Mutations in bestrophin-1 are associated with several retinal
#=GF CC   degenerative disorders. Bestrophins are found in both eukaryotes and
#=GF CC   prokaryotes suggesting an ancient evolutionary origin.

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   Beta_tulip
#=GF AC   CL0845.1
#=GF DE   Beta-tulip superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   37422479
#=GF RT   High-resolution cryo-EM structure of the Pseudomonas bacteriophage
#=GF RT   E217.
#=GF RA   Li F, Hou CD, Lokareddy RK, Yang R, Forti F, Briani F, Cingolani G;
#=GF RL   Nat Commun. 2023;14:4052.
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   33066635
#=GF RT   Keeping It Together: Structures, Functions, and Applications of Viral
#=GF RT   Decoration  Proteins.
#=GF RA   Dedeo CL, Teschke CM, Alexandrescu AT;
#=GF RL   Viruses. 2020;12:1163.
#=GF CC   This superfamily describes a set of phage protein families that adopt
#=GF CC   the beta-tulip fold. The beta-tulip fold is a structural motif found
#=GF CC   primarily in viral decoration proteins that bind to the surfaces of
#=GF CC   phage and viral capsids. It consists of a 5-stranded antiparallel
#=GF CC   beta-barrel with an alpha-helix between strands 3 and 4. One end of
#=GF CC   the barrel is flared open (bloom side) while the other is capped by
#=GF CC   loops (stem side). The beta-tulip motif combines with a mixed
#=GF CC   alpha+beta subdomain to form the full decoration protein structure.
#=GF CC   Three subfamilies of beta-tulip decoration proteins are represented
#=GF CC   by gpD (phage lambda), gp87 (phage P74-26)/gp88 (phage P23-45), and a
#=GF CC   domain of gp8.5 (phage phi29). These proteins form trimers in their
#=GF CC   capsid-bound states, with the bloom side of the beta-tulip
#=GF CC   interacting with the mixed alpha+beta subdomain of neighboring
#=GF CC   protomers to stabilize the trimer. The N-terminal region is involved
#=GF CC   in capsid binding, while the C-terminal domain can form an
#=GF CC   alpha-helical structure. Beta-tulip decoration proteins bind capsids
#=GF CC   at icosahedral symmetry and quasi-symmetry sites, reinforcing weak
#=GF CC   points to provide stability. The rare occurrence of the beta-tulip
#=GF CC   fold outside of viruses, such as in the bacterial protein MoeA,
#=GF CC   suggests horizontal transfer in the evolution of these proteins [2].
#=GF MB   PF09956;
#=GF MB   PF22758;
#=GF MB   PF23968;
#=GF MB   PF23982;
//
# STOCKHOLM 1.0

#=GF AU   Sanchez L
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   12114016
#=GF RT   BRICHOS: a conserved domain in proteins associated with dementia,
#=GF RT   respiratory distress and cancer. 
#=GF RA   Sanchez-Pulido L, Devos D, Valencia A; 
#=GF RL   Trends Biochem Sci 2002;27:329-332.
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   22308375
#=GF RT   High-resolution structure of a BRICHOS domain and its implications
#=GF RT   for  anti-amyloid chaperone activity on lung surfactant protein C.
#=GF RA   Willander H, Askarieh G, Landreh M, Westermark P, Nordling K, Keranen
#=GF RA   H, Hermansson E, Hamvas A, Nogee LM, Bergman T, Saenz A, Casals C,
#=GF RA   Aqvistg J, Jornvall H, Berglund H, Presto J, Knight SD, Johansson J;
#=GF RL   Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A. 2012;109:2325-2329.
#=GF RN   [3]
#=GF RM   36699367
#=GF RT   OAF: a new member of the BRICHOS family.
#=GF RA   Sanchez-Pulido L, Ponting CP;
#=GF RL   Bioinform Adv. 2022;2:vbac087.
#=GF CC   The members of this clan are characterized by being expressed as
#=GF CC   pre-pro-proteins and producing peptides with amyloid properties. This

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   C1q_TNF
#=GF AC   CL0100.13
#=GF DE   C1q and TNF superfamily
#=GF AU   Finn RD
#=GF WK   Tumor_necrosis_factor_superfamily
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   15364058
#=GF RT   C1q and tumor necrosis factor superfamily: modularity and
#=GF RT   versatility. 
#=GF RA   Kishore U, Gaboriaud C, Waters P, Shrive AK, Greenhough TJ, Reid KB,
#=GF RA   Sim RB, Arlaud GJ; 
#=GF RL   Trends Immunol 2004;25:551-561.
#=GF DR   CATH; 2.60.120.40;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 49842;
#=GF CC   The members of the C1q and TNF superfamily are involved in a diverse
#=GF CC   set  of functions, which include: defense, inflammation, apoptosis,
#=GF CC   autoimmunity differentiation, organogenesis, hibernation and
#=GF CC   insulin-resistant obesity [1].  Both C1q and TNF domains form a
#=GF CC   compact jelly-roll beta- sandwich.  The core of these structures are
#=GF CC   conserved between the two families and corresponds to the detectable
#=GF CC   sequence similarity. Proteins containing both of these domains, form
#=GF CC   trimers before they are active.  However, the surfaces of the domains
#=GF CC   are quite different and  this difference is thought to give rise to
#=GF CC   the function difference between the clan members[1].

#=GF ID   C2orf42_C

#=GF CC   uncharacterised protein C2orf42. Domains share a common fold

#=GF CC   are interupted by large insertions.

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   C4-like
#=GF AC   CL0821.1
#=GF DE   C4 zinc binding domain superfamily
#=GF AU   Andreeva A
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   14517549
#=GF RT   Structural determinants of SecB recognition by SecA in bacterial
#=GF RT   protein  translocation.
#=GF RA   Zhou J, Xu Z;
#=GF RL   Nat Struct Biol. 2003;10:942-947.
#=GF CC   This zinc binding domain is found at the C terminus of the SecA
#=GF CC   protein, in the middle of some SWI2 ATPases and also standalone in
#=GF CC   several proteins. It is also found at the N and C terminus of the
#=GF CC   functionally uncharacterised YchJ family suggesting internal
#=GF CC   duplication. This domain coordinates zinc with the CXC and CC pairs.
#=GF CC   In some proteins, the last zinc coordinating resudue is replaced by
#=GF CC   histidine. In SecA, this domain is involved in binding to SecB [1].
#=GF MB   PF21612;

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   CAP
#=GF AC   CL0659.1
#=GF DE   Cysteine-rich secretory proteins
#=GF AU   El-Gebali S
#=GF WK   Cysteine-rich_secretory_protein_superfamily
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   26899679
#=GF RT   Identification of novel CAP superfamily protein members of
#=GF RT   Echinococcus granulosus protoscoleces.
#=GF RA   Silvarrey MC, Echeverria S, Costabile A, Castillo E, Paulino M,
#=GF RA   Esteves A;
#=GF RL   Acta Trop. 2016;158:59-67.
#=GF CC   This is a highly evolutionary diversified protein superfamily whose
#=GF CC   members share specific sequence motifs. For the mammalian members,
#=GF CC   roles in reproduction, development, immune function and several
#=GF CC   pathologies have been proposed. Ant and wasp allergens, snake venom
#=GF CC   toxins and plant antifungal proteins are included in this superfamily
#=GF CC   data suggest that CAP proteins participate in various biological
#=GF CC   activities across phyla. Several signature sequences characterize
#=GF CC   mammalian members of the superfamily: CAP1-2 and CAP3-4, variable
#=GF CC   Cys-rich hinge regions and Ct extensions. Mammalian CAPs include nine
#=GF CC   subfamilies: cysteine rich secretory proteins (CRISPs), glioma
#=GF CC   pathogenesis related-1 (GLIPR1), glioma pathogenesis related-2
#=GF CC   (GLIPR2)/Golgi, associated pathogenesis related-1 (GAPR1), peptidase
#=GF CC   inhibitor 15 (PI15), peptidase inhibitor 16 (PI16), CRISP LCCL domain
#=GF CC   containing 1 and 2 (CRISPLD1/CAPLD1 and CRISPLD2/CAPLD2), mannose
#=GF CC   receptor-like/CP domain, CTL domain containing (CAPCL) and the R3H
#=GF CC   domain-like protein (R3HMDL). CRISP (cysteine rich secretory
#=GF CC   proteins) proteins (venom-related proteins, and proteins produced by
#=GF CC   reproductive organs and cells) and plant-derived pathogenesis related
#=GF CC   proteins are the best characterized. Helminth parasite proteins of
#=GF CC   this superfamily were first reported in hookworms and later in
#=GF CC   related nematodes and platyhelminths. While nematodes' CAP proteins
#=GF CC   are known as Ancylostoma secreted proteins (ASPs), platyhelminths'
#=GF CC   CAP are known as venom allergen-like (VAL) proteins. Some of these
#=GF CC   proteins have been studied as vaccine candidates due to their
#=GF CC   immunogenic and protective properties (e.g. Na-ASP-2, Ov-Asp1,
#=GF CC   Oo-ASP-1) [1].

#=GF CC   that act on adenosine, cytosine, guanine and cytidine, and are 
#=GF CC   collectively known as the deaminase superfamily.  The conserved fold 
#=GF CC   consists of a three-layered alpha/beta/alpha structure with 3 helices
#=GF CC    and 4 strands in the 2134 order [1,2].This superfamily is further 
#=GF CC   divided into two major divisions based on the presence of a  helix
#=GF CC   (helix-4) that renders the terminal strands (strands 4 and 5) either
#=GF CC   parallel to each other in its presence, or anti-parallel  in its
#=GF CC   absence [2]. Structurally, the deaminase-like fold is present in four
#=GF CC   other superfamilies including the JAB-like metalloproteins,  the
#=GF CC   C-terminal AICAR transformylase-catalyzing domains of PurH,  Tm1506
#=GF CC   and the formate dehydrogenase accessory subunit FdhD. The active site
#=GF CC   of the deaminases is composed of three residues  that coordinate a
#=GF CC   zinc ion between conserved helices 2 and 3. The  residues are
#=GF CC   typically found as [HCD]xE and CxxC motifs at the  beginning of
#=GF CC   helices 2 and 3. The zinc ion activates a water  molecule, which
#=GF CC   forms a tetrahderal intermediate with the carbon  atom that is linked
#=GF CC   to the amine group. This is followed by  deamination of the base.

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   CLIP
#=GF AC   CL0678.1
#=GF DE   CLIP domain superfamily
#=GF AU   El-Gebali S
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   26688791
#=GF RT   Clip-domain serine proteases as immune factors in insect hemolymph.
#=GF RA   Kanost MR, Jiang H;
#=GF RL   Curr Opin Insect Sci. 2015;11:47-55.
#=GF CC   CLIP proteases are non-digestive serine proteases present in
#=GF CC   hemolymph of insects and other arthropods. They are composed of one
#=GF CC   or more amino-terminal clip domains followed by a linker sequence and
#=GF CC   a carboxyl-terminal S1A family serine protease domain. Infections can
#=GF CC   stimulate activation of CLIP protease zymogens in hemolymph, with
#=GF CC   specific cleavage at a site at the amino-terminus of the protease
#=GF CC   domain, creating a two-chain form active form of the enzyme, in which
#=GF CC   the clip domain and protease domain remain connected by a disulfide
#=GF CC   bond. Once CLIP proteases are activated, they participate in cascade
#=GF CC   pathways to trigger activation of prophenoloxidase (proPO) or the
#=GF CC   Toll ligand spätzle and are regulated by serpin inhibitors in
#=GF CC   hemolymph plasma. Some members of the CLIP superfamily contain a
#=GF CC   protease domain with mutations of one or more of the catalytic triad
#=GF CC   residues (i.e. pseudoproteases), such that they lack proteolytic
#=GF CC   activity. Such serine protease homologs (SPH) can function as
#=GF CC   cofactors required for proPO activation by an active CLIP protease,
#=GF CC   and they can also negatively regulate the melanization response. Clip
#=GF CC   domain sequences fall into three major groups based on sequence
#=GF CC   similarity and the distance between certain Cys residues. These clip
#=GF CC   domain groups are labeled types 1, 2, and 3, with three subgroups
#=GF CC   identified in type 1 (1a, 1b, 1c). There is a conserved association
#=GF CC   of certain clip domain types with protease domain types, indicating a
#=GF CC   co-evolution of distinct combinations of the clip domain and protease
#=GF CC   domain types. There are conserved Cys1 and Cys5, Cys2 and Cys4, and
#=GF CC   Cys3 and Cys6 which are predicted to form three disulfide bonds [1].
#=GF MB   PF12032;
#=GF MB   PF18322;
#=GF MB   PF18398;
#=GF MB   PF18399;

#=GF ID   CdiI
#=GF AC   CL0964.1
#=GF DE   CdiI-like superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF CC   Members of this clan are bacterial proteins involved in toxin-
#=GF CC   antitoxin systems and bacterial contact-dependent inhibition (CDI)
#=GF CC   defence mechanisms. CDI toxins are expressed by Gram-negative
#=GF CC   bacteria as a mechanism to inhibit growth of neighbouring cells. This
#=GF CC   clan includes CdiI immunity proteins that inhibit CdiA effector
#=GF CC   toxins, structurally related immunity proteins such as those found in
#=GF CC   prophage genomes, and uncharacterised proteins showing sequence or
#=GF CC   structural similarity to known CDI immunity factors. Members adopt
#=GF CC   alpha/beta fold structures and function to neutralise or prevent the
#=GF CC   activity of cognate toxin proteins.
#=GF MB   PF24172;

#=GF RN   [1]

#=GF RT   The diverse CheC-type phosphatases: chemotaxis and beyond.
#=GF RA   Muff TJ, Ordal GW;
#=GF RL   Mol Microbiol. 2008;70:1054-1061.
#=GF DR   SCOP; 103039;
#=GF CC   The chemotactic response regulator superfamily are CheY-P
#=GF CC   phosphatases. Their structure is two intertwined
#=GF CC   alpha-beta-(X)-beta(2) motifs. This superfamily comprises two classes
#=GF CC   of proteins each shown to interact with the chemotaxis response
#=GF CC   regulator CheY: the FliM switch proteins and the CheC-type
#=GF CC   phosphatases [1]. FliM is a component of the flagellar switch found
#=GF CC   across the bacteria and is responsible for binding CheY-P and
#=GF CC   changing the rotational direction of the flagella. The N-terminal
#=GF CC   domain is CheC-like and the C-terminal shares the SpoA domain with
#=GF CC   FliN and FliY. The CheC family is broadly broken down into three
#=GF CC   phosphatase subfamilies: CheC, CheX, and FliY. All three have an
#=GF CC   active site consensus sequence of D/S-X(3)-E-X(2)-N-X(22)-P.
#=GF MB   PF04509;

#=GF ID   Chorimate_bind
#=GF AC   CL0969.1
#=GF DE   Chorismate binding enzyme superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   11371633
#=GF RT   The structures of anthranilate synthase of Serratia marcescens
#=GF RT   crystallized in the presence of (i) its substrates, chorismate and
#=GF RT   glutamine, and a product, glutamate, and (ii) its end-product
#=GF RT   inhibitor, L-tryptophan. 
#=GF RA   Spraggon G, Kim C, Nguyen-Huu X, Yee MC, Yanofsky C, Mills SE; 
#=GF RL   Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A 2001;98:6021-6026.
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   14982443
#=GF RT   Conservation of mechanism in three chorismate-utilizing enzymes.
#=GF RA   He Z., Stigers Lavoie K.D., Bartlett P.A., Toney M.D.;
#=GF RL   J Am Chem Soc. 2004;126:2378-2385.
#=GF CC   This clan contains enzymes that bind and transform chorismate, the
#=GF CC   end-product of the shikimate pathway for biosynthesis of aromatic
#=GF CC   compounds in plants, bacteria, fungi and some parasites. Members
#=GF CC   include anthranilate synthase component I (TrpE), para-aminobenzoate
#=GF CC   synthase component I (PabB), aminodeoxychorismate synthase (ADCS) and
#=GF CC   isochorismate synthase. These homologous enzymes share a conserved
#=GF CC   mechanism involving nucleophile addition to C2 of chorismate in an
#=GF CC   SN2'' process [2]. The enzymes catalyse the initial transformations
#=GF CC   of chorismate in the biosynthesis of tryptophan, p-aminobenzoate,
#=GF CC   folate and enterobactin respectively.  Anthranilate synthase
#=GF CC   structures reveal that the active site lies in the chorismate binding
#=GF CC   domain, with feedback inhibition by tryptophan occurring at a
#=GF CC   distinct site in an adjacent N-terminal domain [1].
#=GF MB   PF00425;
#=GF MB   PF30495;

//
# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   DNA_clamp
#=GF AC   CL0060.12
#=GF DE   DNA clamp superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   12888511
#=GF RT   Evolutionary clues to DNA polymerase III beta clamp structural
#=GF RT   mechanisms. 
#=GF RA   Neuwald AF; 
#=GF RL   Nucleic Acids Res 2003;31:4503-4516.
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   10982878
#=GF RT   PSI-BLAST searches using hidden markov models of structural repeats:
#=GF RT   prediction of an unusual sliding DNA clamp and of beta-propellers in
#=GF RT   UV-damaged DNA-binding protein. 
#=GF RA   Neuwald AF, Poleksic A; 
#=GF RL   Nucleic Acids Res 2000;28:3570-3580.
#=GF DR   SCOP; 55979;
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.70.10.20;
#=GF CC   Sliding DNA clamps are ring-shaped proteins that allow DNA polymerase
#=GF CC   to achieve high processivity during chromosome replication by
#=GF CC   tethering the polymerase catalytic subunit to DNA. All of the
#=GF CC   structures share a 12-fold symmetry around the ring consisting of a
#=GF CC   simple structural repeat, though there is structural divergence in
#=GF CC   some of the repeats. Bacterial beta-clamps contain six repeats per
#=GF CC   subunit with two subunits per ring while the eukaryotic and
#=GF CC   bacteriophage clamps contain four repeats per subunit with three
#=GF CC   subunits per ring. Pairs of these repeats form a domain, which has
#=GF CC   been termed the 'processivity fold'; thus the ring of the sliding
#=GF CC   clamp contains six domains and therefore is often described as having
#=GF CC   6-fold symmetry. A structural representative of a fourth family of
#=GF CC   processivity fold proteins, namely the herpes simplex virus UL42
#=GF CC   protein, is also available. UL42 does not form a ring-shaped clamp,
#=GF CC   however, but rather functions as a monomer and interacts with DNA
#=GF CC   quite differently than do sliding clamps; it has been suggested that

#=GF CC   domain (DD); the death effector domain (DED) and the caspase
#=GF CC   recruitment domain (CARD).  All of the members perform a pivotal role
#=GF CC   in signalling events that regulate apoptosis.   Protein-protein

#=GF AU   Mistry J, Bateman A
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   9023221
#=GF RT   The terminal quinol oxidase of the hyperthermophilic archaeon
#=GF RT   Acidianus ambivalens exhibits a novel subunit structure and gene
#=GF RT   organization. 
#=GF RA   Purschke WG, Schmidt CL, Petersen A, Schafer G; 
#=GF RL   J Bacteriol 1997;179:1344-1353.
#=GF CC   The families in this clan are all membrane proteins.  The DoxD family
#=GF CC   is found on enzymes involved in elemental sulphur oxidation [1].  The
#=GF CC   other families in this clan are poorly characterised. In general many
#=GF CC   of the families in this superfamily consist of four integral membrane
#=GF CC   alpha helices and form homodimeric conformations. A few families
#=GF CC   appear to contain two copies of the domain. Many members of the
#=GF CC   superfamily have a conserved histidine in the final alpha helix. Also
#=GF CC   the second and third helices are placed at an angle which leaves a
#=GF CC   cavity between them which presumably binds to an as yet unknown
#=GF CC   ligand.

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   GHMP_C
#=GF AC   CL0677.1
#=GF DE   GHMP C-terminal domain superfamily
#=GF AU   El-Gebali S
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   21474160
#=GF RT   The role of the active site residues in human galactokinase:
#=GF RT   implications for the mechanisms of GHMP kinases.
#=GF RA   Megarity CF, Huang M, Warnock C, Timson DJ;
#=GF RL   Bioorg Chem. 2011;39:120-126.
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   15544330
#=GF RT   Molecular functions of conserved aspects of the GHMP kinase family.
#=GF RA   Andreassi JL 2nd, Leyh TS;
#=GF RL   Biochemistry. 2004;43:14594-14601.
#=GF RN   [3]
#=GF RM   11188689
#=GF RT   Structure and mechanism of homoserine kinase: prototype for the GHMP
#=GF RT   kinase superfamily.
#=GF RA   Zhou T, Daugherty M, Grishin NV, Osterman AL, Zhang H;
#=GF RL   Structure. 2000;8:1247-1257.
#=GF CC   The GHMP kinases are a group of structurally related small kinases
#=GF CC   which are mostly involved in intermediary metabolism. The group is
#=GF CC   named after four members - galactokinase, homserine kinase,
#=GF CC   mevalonate kinase and phosphomevalonate kinase. It also includes
#=GF CC   4-(cytidine 5'-diphospho)-2-C-methyl-D-erythritol kinase, isopentenyl
#=GF CC   monophosphate kinase, mevalonate diphosphate decarboxylase, archaeal
#=GF CC   shikimate kinases, N-acetyl galactosamine kinase, L-threonine kinase,
#=GF CC   glucuronokinase and galacturonic acid kinase. In addition to these
#=GF CC   enzymes, two proteins without catalytic activity - the Saccharomyces
#=GF CC   cerevisiae transcriptional regulator, Gal3p and the Caenorhabditis
#=GF CC   elegans sex fate determining protein XOL-1 have similar folds [1]. Of
#=GF CC   the seven enzymes, six kinases and one decarboxylase, two have been
#=GF CC   linked to human disease, two are involved in the biosynthesis of
#=GF CC   aromatic and nonaromatic amino acids, folates, and ubiquinones, one
#=GF CC   is needed to deliver galactose to the glycolytic pathway, and four
#=GF CC   are essential for either the mevalonate- or non-mevalonate-dependent
#=GF CC   synthesis of isoprenoids. The structural scaffold of the family has
#=GF CC   been evolutionary maintained, while the residues have been allowed to
#=GF CC   drift, however there are highly conserved solvent-accessible residues
#=GF CC   that are characteristic of the GHMP kinase family [2]. This clan
#=GF CC   represents the structurally conserved C-terminal domain which has a
#=GF CC   central alpha-beta plait fold and an insertion of four helices.
#=GF CC   Together with the N-terminal fold it creates a nucleotide binding
#=GF CC   fold with the active site located at the domain interface [3].
#=GF MB   PF08544;
#=GF MB   PF09109;
#=GF MB   PF18376;
#=GF MB   PF21433;

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   HAMP
#=GF AC   CL0681.1
#=GF DE   HAMP domains
#=GF AU   El-Gebali S
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   23424282
#=GF RT   HAMP domain conformers that propagate opposite signals in bacterial
#=GF RT   chemoreceptors.
#=GF RA   Airola MV, Sukomon N, Samanta D, Borbat PP, Freed JH, Watts KJ, Crane
#=GF RA   BR;
#=GF RL   PLoS Biol. 2013;11:e1001479.
#=GF CC   The ability of single-celled organisms to sense, respond to, and
#=GF CC   adapt to their changing environment requires receptor proteins to
#=GF CC   convert extracellular signals into cellular responses. Central to
#=GF CC   many of these signal transduction systems are HAMP (histidine
#=GF CC   kinases, adenylyl cyclases, methylaccepting proteins, and other
#=GF CC   prokaryotic signaling proteins) domains, which act to couple sensory
#=GF CC   and output domains in different receptor proteins. In transmembrane
#=GF CC   receptors, HAMP domains connect to transmembrane helices entering the
#=GF CC   cytoplasm and translate chemical, photo, and thermo stimuli to the
#=GF CC   output of cytoplasmic catalytic domains (mainly histidine kinases,
#=GF CC   adenylyl cyclases, methyl-accepting chemotaxis proteins [MCPs], and
#=GF CC   phosphatases). Deletion of HAMP domains disrupts the link between
#=GF CC   input and output units, generating receptors incapable of switching
#=GF CC   activity states upon stimulation. HAMP domains are small modules,
#=GF CC   approximately 50 amino acids, that dimerize to form an entirely
#=GF CC   parallel four-helix bundle with two helices (AS1 and AS2) supplied
#=GF CC   from each subunit. The AS1 and AS2 helices form a seven-residue
#=GF CC   pattern characteristic of coiled coils, termed a heptad repeat [1].
#=GF MB   PF18575;
#=GF MB   PF00672;
#=GF MB   PF18947;
#=GF MB   PF21927;

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   HUH
#=GF AC   CL0481.3
#=GF DE   His-hydrophobic-His tranposase Y1,Y2 superfamily
#=GF AU   Coggill P
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   16340015
#=GF RT   Crystal structure of a metal ion-bound IS200 transposase.
#=GF RA   Lee HH, Yoon JY, Kim HS, Kang JY, Kim KH, Kim DJ, Ha JY, Mikami B,
#=GF RA   Yoon HJ, Suh SW; 
#=GF RL   J Biol Chem. 2006;281:4261-4266.
#=GF DR   SCOP; 143422;
#=GF CC   This superfamily consists of families of transposases that use
#=GF CC   tyrosine as nucleophiles and contain a conserved HUH motif where U is
#=GF CC   a non-conserved hydrophobic residue. Particular examples are the Y1
#=GF CC   and Y2 transposases. Y1 transposases come from the IS608 group and
#=GF CC   the Y2 from the IS91 group. These transposases are nucleases involved
#=GF CC   in initiating rolling-circle plasmid replication, in initiating
#=GF CC   replication of certain single-strand (ss) DNA viruses (Rep proteins),
#=GF CC   and in initiation of conjugative plasmid transfer (relaxases). The
#=GF CC   IS91 transposase contains two of the three conserved protein sequence
#=GF CC   motifs identified in RCR proteins, a His-hydrophobic-His (HUH) motif
#=GF CC   required for metal ion binding, and a YxxxY motif containing two
#=GF CC   conserved catalytic tyrosine residues. The HUH motif participates in
#=GF CC   coordinating divalent metal ions essential for catalysis.  This clan
#=GF CC   is also known as the Transposase IS200-like whose members contain
#=GF CC   extra N-terminal hairpin and C-terminal helix, both or which are
#=GF CC   involved in dimerisation; there can be helix-swapping in the dimer.

#=GF CC   The histidine phosphatase superfamily is so named because catalysis
#=GF CC   centres on a conserved His residue that is transiently phosphorylated
#=GF CC   during the catalytic cycle.  Other conserved residues contribute to a
#=GF CC   'phosphate pocket' and interact with the phospho group of substrate
#=GF CC   before, during and after its transfer to the His residue.   Structure
#=GF CC   and sequence analyses show that different families contribute
#=GF CC   different additional residues to the 'phosphate pocket' and, more
#=GF CC   surprisingly, differ in the position, in sequence and in three
#=GF CC   dimensions, of a catalytically essential acidic residue.  The
#=GF CC   superfamily may be divided into two main branches [1].

#=GF RT   dehydratases.

#=GF RL   BMC Bioinformatics 2004;5:109-109.
#=GF DR   SCOP; 54637;
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.10.129.10;
#=GF CC   The HotDog fold was first observed in the structure of Escherichia
#=GF CC   coli beta-hydroxydecanoyl thiol ester dehydratase (FabA), where
#=GF CC   Leesong et al. noticed that each subunit of this dimeric enzyme
#=GF CC   contained a mixed alpha + beta 'hot dog' fold. They described the
#=GF CC   seven-stranded antiparallel beta-sheet as the 'bun', which wraps
#=GF CC   around a five-turn alpha-helical 'sausage', This superfamily contains
#=GF CC   a diverse range of enzymes. Membership includes numerous prokaryotic,
#=GF CC   archaeal and eukaryotic proteins involved in several related, but
#=GF CC   distinct, catalytic activities, from metabolic roles such as
#=GF CC   thioester hydrolysis in fatty acid metabolism, to degradation of
#=GF CC   phenylacetic acid and the environmental pollutant 4-chlorobenzoate.
#=GF CC   The superfamily also includes FapR, a non-catalytic bacterial
#=GF CC   homologue that is involved in transcriptional regulation of fatty
#=GF CC   acid biosynthesis [1].

#=GF CC   This clan represents the C-terminal domain of some KCTD family
#=GF CC   proteins, in particular KCTD1, KCTD4, KCTD6, KCTD8, KCTD11, KCTD12,
#=GF CC   KCTD15, KCTD16, KCTD21 etc. Genetic alternations in KCTD family
#=GF CC   members have been associated with neurodevelopmental disorders,
#=GF CC   epilepsy, autism, schizophrenia, movement disorders, obesity, and
#=GF CC   several cancers [1]. This domain follows the BTB domain, common to
#=GF CC   all KCTD proteins, but it is more divergent and is found only in the
#=GF CC   proteins listed above. These proteins, with exception of KCTD4,
#=GF CC   define distinct phylogenetic groups referred to as group D, E and F.
#=GF CC   Members adopt an alpha/beta structure composed of a five-stranded
#=GF CC   antiparallel beta-sheet with a strand order 32154 and two
#=GF CC   alpha-helices packed on it. The length of strands may vary amongst
#=GF CC   the members.
#=GF MB   PF19323;
#=GF MB   PF19329;
#=GF MB   PF20871;

#=GF ID   KH

#=GF DE   K-Homology (KH) domain Superfamily
#=GF AU   Finn RD
#=GF WK   KH_domain
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.30.70.210;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 54791;
#=GF CC   The KH domain is thought to be the second most prevalent RNA  binding
#=GF CC   motif in proteins.  The motif is characterised by a  conserved
#=GF CC   GXXXGXXG in the middle of the domain.  Structures of KH reveal that
#=GF CC   the KH domain is arranged as either a  beta-alpha-alpha-beta-beta
#=GF CC   (mini-KH domain) or  beta-alpha-alpha-beta-beta-alpha (maxi-KH
#=GF CC   domain).  The secondary elements are separated by at least four loop
#=GF CC   segments. The second loop is located between beta-1 and al  The KH
#=GF CC   domain can be found either as single or multiple copies.  The KH
#=GF CC   domain usually binds RNA as a multimer.

#=GF CC   comprising three main stages. The second stage implies the addition
#=GF CC   of a short polypeptide chain to the UDPMurNAc. Mur ligases are
#=GF CC   topologically similar to one another, they consist f three domains:
#=GF CC   an N-terminal Rossmann-fold domain responsible for binding the
#=GF CC   UDPMurNAc substrate; a central domain (similar to ATP-binding domains
#=GF CC   of several ATPases and GTPases); and a C-terminal domain that binds
#=GF CC   the incoming amino acid [1-3]. Residues found in the three domains
#=GF CC   are involved in the catalytic process. This C-terminal domain also
#=GF CC   plays a critical role in capping the ATP binding site and inserting a
#=GF CC   loop into the active site to orient the amino acid [1]. This
#=GF CC   superfamily also includes the C-terminal domain of folylpolyglutamate
#=GF CC   synthase and cyanophycin synthetase.
#=GF MB   PF02875;

#=GF ID   NADP_Rossmann
#=GF AC   CL0063.25
#=GF DE   FAD/NAD(P)-binding Rossmann fold Superfamily
#=GF AU   Finn RD
#=GF WK   Rossmann_fold
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   11812158
#=GF RT   The geometry of domain combination in proteins. 
#=GF RA   Bashton M, Chothia C; 
#=GF RL   J Mol Biol 2002;315:927-939.
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.40.50.720;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 51984;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 51904;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 51971;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 51734;
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.40.50.1320;
#=GF CC   A class of redox enzymes are two domain proteins. One domain, termed
#=GF CC   the catalytic domain, confers substrate specificity and the precise
#=GF CC   reaction of the enzyme.  The other domain, which is common to this
#=GF CC   class of redox enzymes, is a Rossmann-fold domain. The Rossmann
#=GF CC   domain binds nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide (NAD+) and it is this
#=GF CC   cofactor that reversibly accepts a hydride ion, which is lost or
#=GF CC   gained by the substrate in the redox reaction.  Rossmann domains have
#=GF CC   an alpha/beta fold, which has a central beta sheet, with
#=GF CC   approximately five alpha helices found surrounding the beta sheet.The
#=GF CC   strands forming the beta sheet are found in the following
#=GF CC   characteristic order 654123. The inter sheet crossover of the stands
#=GF CC   in the sheet form the NAD+ binding site [1].  In some more distantly
#=GF CC   relate Rossmann domains the NAD+ cofactor is replaced by the
#=GF CC   functionally similar cofactor FAD.

#=GF CC   In the N-terminal nucleophile aminohydrolases (Ntn hydrolases) the
#=GF CC   N-terminal residue provides two catalytic groups, nucleophile and
#=GF CC   proton donor. These enzymes use the side chain of the amino-terminal
#=GF CC   residue, incorporated in a beta-sheet, as the nucleophile in the
#=GF CC   catalytic attack at the carbonyl carbon. The nucleophile is cysteine
#=GF CC   in GAT, serine in penicillin acylase, and threonine in the
#=GF CC   proteasome. All the enzymes share an unusual fold in which the
#=GF CC   nucleophile and other catalytic groups occupy equivalent sites. This
#=GF CC   fold provides both the capacity for nucleophilic attack and the
#=GF CC   possibility of autocatalytic processing [1].

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   OML_zippers
#=GF AC   CL0590.2
#=GF DE   Major outer membrane lipoprotein zipper superfamily
#=GF AU   Coggill P
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   25165619
#=GF RT   Insights on the structure and stability of Licanantase: a trimeric
#=GF RT   acid-stable coiled-coil lipoprotein from Acidithiobacillus
#=GF RT   thiooxidans.
#=GF RA   Abarca F, Gutierrez-Maldonado SE, Parada P, Martinez P, Maass A,
#=GF RA   Perez-Acle T;
#=GF RL   PeerJ. 2014;2:e457.
#=GF DR   SCOP; 58042;
#=GF DR   CATH; 1.20.5.190;
#=GF CC   This is a superfamily of short outer membrane lipoporteins that are
#=GF CC   coiled-coils of alpha-helices. The families included are both leucine
#=GF CC   and alanine zippers. A zipper motif is a heptad or seven-residue
#=GF CC   repeat, represented by X..X...X..X...X..X...X..X...X..X, etc, where
#=GF CC   the X is hydrophobic residue, usually a leucine or alanine, and the
#=GF CC   intermediate residues are largely polar or hydrophilic; that is, the
#=GF CC   responsible residues occur at positions 1 and 4 in the heptad.
#=GF CC   Monomers combine together into dimers and higher order structures.
#=GF CC   Coiled-coil motifs are ubiquitous mediators of specific
#=GF CC   protein-protein interactions, in this case outer membrane 'tubes', by
#=GF CC   forming interlocking hydrophobic seams between the alpha-helical
#=GF CC   chains [1].
#=GF MB   PF04728;
#=GF MB   PF11839;
#=GF MB   PF09403;

#=GF ID   PRD
#=GF AC   CL0166.12
#=GF DE   PRD domain superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF DR   SCOP; 63520;
#=GF CC   The PRD domain (for PTS Regulation Domain), is the phosphorylatable
#=GF CC   regulatory domain found in bacterial transcriptional antiterminator
#=GF CC   of the BglG family as well as in activators such as MtlR and LevR.
#=GF CC   The PRD domain is phosphorylated on a conserved histidine residue.
#=GF CC   PRD-containing proteins are involved in the regulation of catabolic
#=GF CC   operons in Gram+ and Gram- bacteria and are often characterised by a
#=GF CC   short N-terminal effector domain that binds to either RNA (CAT-RBD
#=GF CC   for antiterminators (Pfam:PF03123, see also comments for this
#=GF CC   family)) or DNA (for activators), and a duplicated PRD module which
#=GF CC   is phosphorylated on conserved histidines by the sugar
#=GF CC   phosphotransferase system (PTS) in response to the availability of
#=GF CC   carbon source. The phosphorylations are thought to modify the
#=GF CC   stability of the dimeric proteins and thereby the RNA- or DNA-binding
#=GF CC   activity of the effector domain.
#=GF MB   PF00874;
#=GF MB   PF08270;

#=GF CC   always found associated with prokaryotic CRISPRs, themselves a family
#=GF CC   of clustered regularly interspaced short palindromic repeats, DNA
#=GF CC   repeats found in nearly half of all bacterial and archaeal genomes.
#=GF CC   These DNA repeat regions have a remarkably regular structure: unique
#=GF CC   sequences of constant size, called spacers, sit between each pair of
#=GF CC   repeats [1]. It has been shown that the CRISPRs are virus-derived
#=GF CC   sequences acquired by the host to enable them to resist viral
#=GF CC   infection. The Cas proteins from the host use the CRISPRs to mediate
#=GF CC   an antiviral response. After transcription of the CRISPR, a complex
#=GF CC   of Cas proteins termed Cascade cleaves a CRISPR RNA precursor in each
#=GF CC   repeat and retains the cleavage products containing the virus-derived
#=GF CC   sequence. Assisted by the helicase Cas3, these mature CRISPR RNAs
#=GF CC   then serve as small guide RNAs that enable Cascade to interfere with
#=GF CC   virus proliferation [2]. Cas5 contains an endonuclease motif, whose
#=GF CC   inactivation leads to loss of resistance, even in the presence of
#=GF CC   phage-derived spacers [3].

#=GF ID   Rhomboid-like

#=GF DE   Integral membrane protein / protease
#=GF AU   Mistry J
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   12620104
#=GF RT   The rhomboids: a nearly ubiquitous family of intramembrane serine
#=GF RT   proteases that probably evolved by multiple ancient horizontal gene
#=GF RT   transfers. 
#=GF RA   Koonin EV, Makarova KS, Rogozin IB, Davidovic L, Letellier MC,
#=GF RA   Pellegrini L; 
#=GF RL   Genome Biol 2003;4:R19.
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   8631297
#=GF RT   Der1, a novel protein specifically required for endoplasmic reticulum
#=GF RT   degradation in yeast. 
#=GF RA   Knop M, Finger A, Braun T, Hellmuth K, Wolf DH; 
#=GF RL   EMBO J 1996;15:753-763.
#=GF CC   This clan contains proteins from both bacteria and eukaryotes. The
#=GF CC   Rhomboid protein is an intramembrane serine protease which is
#=GF CC   involved in epidermal growth factor (EGF)-dependent signalling
#=GF CC   pathways [1]. The DER1 family is involved in degradation of misfolded
#=GF CC   ER proteins [2].

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   Ribokinase
#=GF AC   CL0118.12
#=GF DE   Ribokinase-like superfamily
#=GF AU   Bateman A
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   15458630
#=GF RT   Crystal structure of an aminoimidazole riboside kinase from
#=GF RT   Salmonella enterica: implications for the evolution of the ribokinase
#=GF RT   superfamily. 
#=GF RA   Zhang Y, Dougherty M, Downs DM, Ealick SE; 
#=GF RL   Structure (Camb) 2004;12:1809-1821.
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.40.1190.20;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 53613;
#=GF CC   All of these enzymes are phosphotransferases that have an alcohol
#=GF CC   group as an acceptor (EC:2.7.1.-). However,
#=GF CC   4-amino-5-hydroxymethyl-2-methylpyrimidine phosphate kinase (HMPP
#=GF CC   kinase) catalyses two phosphorylation reactions: one to a
#=GF CC   hydroxymethyl group of hydroxymethyl pyrimidine (HMP) and the second
#=GF CC   to the phosphomethyl group of HMPP [1]. The common structural feature
#=GF CC   for the enzymes in this superfamily is a central eight-stranded sheet
#=GF CC   that is flanked by eight structurally conserved helices, five on one
#=GF CC   side and three on the other [1]. The active site is located in a
#=GF CC   shallow groove along one edge of the sheet, with the phosphate
#=GF CC   acceptor hydroxyl group and -phosphate of ATP close together in the
#=GF CC   middle of the groove, and substrate and ATP binding at the ends [1].
#=GF MB   PF00294;
#=GF MB   PF04587;
#=GF MB   PF02110;
#=GF MB   PF01256;
#=GF MB   PF08543;
#=GF MB   PF25270;

#=GF CC   Structures of SAM domains reveal a common five helical structure.

#=GF CC   widespread function is in domain-domain interactions.   The SAM

#=GF CC   translation control element (Rfam:RF00161) [3]. This clan currently
#=GF CC   only represents the diverse SAM domain family and does not contain

#=GF CC   performing either an assembly or regulatory role.               For
#=GF CC   example, SH3 domains in the Grb2 adaptor protein  are essential for
#=GF CC   protein-protein interactions and                              signal
#=GF CC   transduction in the p21 Ras-dependent growth factor signaling
#=GF CC   pathway.  Alternatively, SH3 performs a regulatory role in the Src
#=GF CC   family of tyrosine kinases. SH3 domains bind a variety of peptide
#=GF CC   ligands, many of which  contain a PxxP motif.  This PxxP motif is
#=GF CC   flanked by different specificity elements [1].   Structures of SH3
#=GF CC   domains, both free and ligand complexed, have provided insights into
#=GF CC   the mechanism of ligand recognition. The SH3 fold consists of two
#=GF CC   anti-parallel beta sheets that lie  at right angles to each other. 
#=GF CC   Within the fold, there are two variable loops, referred to as RT and
#=GF CC   n-Src loops.  When SH3 binds to its ligand, the proline rich ligand
#=GF CC   adopts a PPII helix conformation, with the PPII helix structure
#=GF CC   recognised by a pair of grooves on the surface of the SH3 domain that
#=GF CC    bind turns of the helix.          The SH3 grooves  are formed by a
#=GF CC   series of nearly parallel, well-conserved aromatic residues [1].

#=GF AU   El-Gebali S

#=GF RT   The TACC proteins: TACC-ling microtubule dynamics and centrosome
#=GF RT   function.
#=GF RA   Peset I, Vernos I;
#=GF RL   Trends Cell Biol. 2008;18:379-388.
#=GF CC   TACC proteins are present in different organisms, ranging from yeasts
#=GF CC   to mammals. For example, TACC proteins can be found in:
#=GF CC   Caenorhabditis elegans (TAC-1), Drosophila melanogaster (D-TACC),
#=GF CC   Xenopus laevis (Maskin), and in the fission yeast Schizosaccharomyces
#=GF CC   pombe (Alp7 also known as Mia1p); by contrast, mammals have three
#=GF CC   such proteins (TACC1, TACC2 [also known as AZU-1 and ECTACC] and
#=GF CC   TACC3 [also known as AINT and ERIC1]). Alternative splicing further
#=GF CC   increases the complexity of the TACC protein family in mammals and
#=GF CC   flies . The first member of the TACC family to be discovered was
#=GF CC   identified in a search of genomic regions that are amplified in
#=GF CC   breast cancer. It was named transforming acidic coiled-coil 1 (TACC1)
#=GF CC   because of its highly acidic nature, the presence of a predicted
#=GF CC   coiled-coil domain at its C terminus (now known as the TACC domain),
#=GF CC   and its ability to promote cellular transformation. one of the
#=GF CC   extensively characterised TACC proteins, Maskin acts as a factor
#=GF CC   involved in the regulation of mRNA translation during maturation of
#=GF CC   Xenopus oocytes. Other TACC family members have also been implicated
#=GF CC   in various events related to gene regulation, including the
#=GF CC   regulation of translation, RNA maturation and gene expression.
#=GF CC   Furthermore, TACC proteins have a role in regulating microtubule
#=GF CC   assembly such as D-TACC which acts as a Drosophila
#=GF CC   microtubule-associated and centrosomal protein required for
#=GF CC   centrosome activity and microtubule assembly during mitosis [1].

#=GF CC   THUMP domain, named after THioUridine synthases, RNA Methylases and
#=GF CC   Pseudouridine synthases, is a module of 100-110 amino acid residues
#=GF CC   which is involved RNA metabolism. It is shared by enzymes involved in
#=GF CC   at least three unrelated types of RNA-modification, namely
#=GF CC   methylation, pseudouridylation and thiouridylation. It can occur in

#=GF ID   TRADD-N
#=GF AC   CL0723.1
#=GF DE   TRADD-N domain superfamily
#=GF AU   Aravind L, Iyer LM
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   34061031
#=GF RT   Bacterial death and TRADD-N domains help define novel apoptosis and
#=GF RT   immunity mechanisms shared by prokaryotes and metazoans.
#=GF RA   Kaur G, Iyer LM, Burroughs AM, Aravind L;
#=GF RL   Elife. 2021; [Epub ahead of print]
#=GF RN   [2]
#=GF RM   10911999
#=GF RT   Solution structure of N-TRADD and characterization of the interaction
#=GF RT   of N-TRADD  and C-TRAF2, a key step in the TNFR1 signaling pathway.
#=GF RA   Tsao DH, McDonagh T, Telliez JB, Hsu S, Malakian K, Xu GY, Lin LL;
#=GF RL   Mol Cell. 2000;5:1051-1057.
#=GF CC   This superfamily (N-terminal region of TRADD, TRADD-N) includes
#=GF CC   adaptor domains that constitute a signalling bridge linking effector
#=GF CC   deployment to invader-sensing in multicellular bacterial and metazoan
#=GF CC   counter-invader systems. TRADD-N is structurally related to the
#=GF CC   small-molecule-binding ACT domain (RRM-like fold) [1] and consists of
#=GF CC   an alpha/beta sandwich where the beta-strands form an antiparallel
#=GF CC   beta-sheet [2].

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   TRAP
#=GF AC   CL0757.1
#=GF DE   Transmembrane adaptor protein class
#=GF AU   Bateman A, Ponamareva I
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   24440308
#=GF RT   Palmitoylated transmembrane adaptor proteins in leukocyte signaling.
#=GF RA   Stepanek O, Draber P, Horejsi V;
#=GF RL   Cell Signal. 2014;26:895-902.
#=GF CC   Transmembrane adaptor proteins (TRAPs) are structurally related
#=GF CC   proteins that have no enzymatic function, but enable inducible
#=GF CC   recruitment of effector molecules to the plasma membrane, usually in
#=GF CC   a phosphorylation dependent manner. Numerous surface receptors employ
#=GF CC   TRAPs for either propagation or negative regulation of the signal
#=GF CC   transduction. Several TRAPs (LAT, NTAL, PAG, LIME, PRR7, SCIMP,
#=GF CC   LST1/A, and putatively GAPT) are known to be palmitoylated that could
#=GF CC   facilitate their localization in lipid rafts or tetraspanin enriched
#=GF CC   microdomains.
#=GF MB   PF15234;

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   TRASH
#=GF AC   CL0175.11
#=GF DE   TRASH superfamily
#=GF AU   Fenech M
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   12713899
#=GF RT   TRASH: a novel metal-binding domain predicted to be involved in
#=GF RT   heavy-metal sensing, trafficking and resistance. 
#=GF RA   Ettema TJ, Huynen MA, de Vos WM, van der Oost J; 
#=GF RL   Trends Biochem Sci 2003;28:170-173.
#=GF CC   TRASH-like domains contain well-conserved cysteine residues that are
#=GF CC   thought to be involved in metal coordination. These domains are thus
#=GF CC   expected to be involved in metal trafficking and heavy-metal
#=GF CC   resistance. It has been suggested that the members adopt a
#=GF CC   'treble-clef' fold, with 3/4 beta strands preceding a C-terminal
#=GF CC   alpha helix [1].
#=GF MB   PF04438;
#=GF MB   PF01753;

#=GF CC   function either degradatively, in the beta-oxidation  pathway of
#=GF CC   fatty acids, or biosynthetically. Biosynthetic thiolases  catalyse
#=GF CC   the formation of acetoacetyl-CoA from two molecules of  acetyl-CoA .

#=GF CC   assembly patterns in biological systems and is the first step  in a
#=GF CC   wide range of biosynthetic pathways [1].  Thiolase are usually
#=GF CC   dimeric or tetrameric enzymes.  Within each monomer there are two
#=GF CC   similar domains related by pseudo dyad.  The N-terminal of these two
#=GF CC   domains contains a large insertion of about 100 amino  acids.

#=GF CC   systems connected to the appearance of Alzheimer’s, Huntington’s,

#=GF CC   classes, corresponding to the polyproline motif with which they
#=GF CC   interact: class 1 with PPxY motifs, class 2 with PPPL/R motifs, class
#=GF CC   3 with (PxxGMxPP)*2 motifs, and class 4 with (pS/pT)P motifs

#=GF CC   show structural similarity to the Sm-like SH3 beta-barrel fold. The
#=GF CC   WYL domain was first named for three conserved amino acids, however
#=GF CC   this motif was subsequently shown to be found only in a small subset
#=GF CC   of examples of this clan. The conservation pattern more widely
#=GF CC   applicable across the clan includes four basic residues and a
#=GF CC   position often occupied by a cysteine. These residues are predicted 
#=GF CC   to line a ligand-binding groove similar to that found in Sm-like SH3 
#=GF CC   beta-barrels. WYL domains often occur in two copies in the same
#=GF CC   protein, or are encoded alongside neighbouring genes that encode
#=GF CC   multi-WYL domain proteins, and it has been suggested that they form
#=GF CC   toroidal multimeric assemblies. WYL domains are often associated with
#=GF CC   various predicted DNA-binding N-terminal wHTH domains, but may also
#=GF CC   exist as fusions to several enzymatic domains.

#=GF CC   three closely packed beta-sheets. Some member proteins are chorismate
#=GF CC   mutases, others are endoribonucleases active on single-stranded mRNA.
#=GF CC   It is the highly conserved YjgF/YER057c/UK114 protein superfamily.
#=GF CC   Homologues of this protein occur in eubacteria, archaea, and
#=GF CC   eukaryotes. Proteins are functionally diverse and are involved in a
#=GF CC   variety of enzymatic and non-enzymatic functions. The high sequence
#=GF CC   and structural similarity between members of this protein superfamily
#=GF CC   offer an example of minimalistic changes leading to functional
#=GF CC   diversification. This feature is best exemplified by the three close
#=GF CC   homologues of YjgF proteins in mammals (human, rat, and goat) with
#=GF CC   sequence identity better than 85%. These homologues perform different
#=GF CC   functions, including tumour antigen activity in the goat homologue,
#=GF CC   translation inhibition in the human and rat homologues (hp14.5 and
#=GF CC   rp14.5), endoribonuclease activity in rp14.5, calpain activation in
#=GF CC   the bovine homologue, molecular chaperone activity in DUK114, and
#=GF CC   involvement in the regulation of purine and removal of toxic
#=GF CC   metabolites in YjgF7, and involvement in isoleucine biosynthetic
#=GF CC   pathways (YjgF, YER057c, Ibm1). In addition, members of this protein
#=GF CC   family have also been shown to regulate mitochondrial maintenance
#=GF CC   (Ibm1) in yeast. Proteins from the YjgF family in plants are involved
#=GF CC   in photosynthesis and chromoplastogenesis (CHRD).

# STOCKHOLM 1.0
#=GF ID   tRNA_synt_II
#=GF AC   CL0040.17
#=GF DE   Class II aminoacyl-tRNA and Biotin synthetases
#=GF AU   Finn RD
#=GF RN   [1]
#=GF RM   10447505
#=GF RT   Evolution of aminoacyl-tRNA synthetases--analysis of unique domain
#=GF RT   architectures and phylogenetic trees reveals a complex history of
#=GF RT   horizontal gene transfer events. 
#=GF RA   Wolf YI, Aravind L, Grishin NV, Koonin EV; 
#=GF RL   Genome Res 1999;9:689-710.
#=GF DR   CATH; 3.40.510.10;
#=GF DR   SCOP; 52375;
#=GF CC   Aminoacyl-tRNA synthetases are key components of the protein
#=GF CC   translation  machinery that catalyse two basic reactions. First, the
#=GF CC   activation of  amino acids via the formation of aminoacyl adenylates
#=GF CC   and second, linking the activated amino acid to the cognate tRNAs.
#=GF CC   The aminoacyl-tRNA synthetases generate AMP as the second end product
#=GF CC   of this reaction, which differentiates them from the majority of
#=GF CC   ATP-dependent enzymes that produce ADP. In addition, there is a
#=GF CC   specific  aminoacyl-tRNA synthetases for each of the 20 amino acids
#=GF CC   and there are  two structurally distinct classes of aminoacyl-tRNA
#=GF CC   synthetases, each                 encompassing 10 different
#=GF CC   specificities. The two classes have alternative modes of
#=GF CC   aminoacylation: class I aminoacylate the 2'OH of the cognate tRNA;
#=GF CC   class II aminoacylate 3'OH (with the exception of PheRS). Each class
#=GF CC   contain a conserved core domain  that is involved in ATP binding and
#=GF CC   hydrolysis and combines with  additional domains that determine the
#=GF CC   specificity of interactions with         the cognate amino acid and
#=GF CC   tRNA.  The class II core domain consist of a mixed-beta sheet,
#=GF CC   similar to that found in the biotin synthetases, hence why this
#=GF CC   family has also been included in  this clan.  The core domain
#=GF CC   contains three modestly conserved motifs  that are responsible for
#=GF CC   ATP binding.  The class II aminoacyl-tRNA  synthetases can contain
#=GF CC   additional nested domains, found inserted in the loops of the core
#=GF CC   domain [1] (and  reference therein).
#=GF MB   PF03099;
#=GF MB   PF01409;
#=GF MB   PF03590;
#=GF MB   PF00152;
#=GF MB   PF00587;
#=GF MB   PF01411;
#=GF MB   PF02091;
#=GF MB   PF13393;
#=GF MB   PF16917;
#=GF MB   PF04017;
#=GF MB   PF17759;
#=GF MB   PF21948;

#=GF ID   zf-HC3
#=GF AC   CL0924.1
#=GF DE   HC3 zinc-finger superfamily
#=GF AU   Andreeva A
#=GF CC   This clan represents unusual type of HC3 zinc-finger domains. These
#=GF CC   domains contain invariant histidine followed by three cysteine
#=GF CC   residues. The histidine residue is located at a strand and one of the
#=GF CC   cysteine residues on a turn while the C-terminal pair of cysteines is
#=GF CC   on a turn of an alpha-helix. The high resolution structure of a
#=GF CC   member Svir_20580 from Saccharomonospora viridis is available
#=GF CC   (PDB:3RMS).
